PERSONNEL OF THE INSURANCE DEPARTMENT 

June 30, 1964 Arizona law prescribes comprehensive insurance regulation in the public interest. Such requires a continually active supervision and maintenance of timely and accurate records. 

Consequently, the Insurance Department has employed and trained a staff of specialists together with competent assistants and clerks to perform essential tasks. As the state continues to grow and to broaden the areas of insurance regulation, the Department too must expand. On June 30, 1964, Departmental officers and employees were:

ACCOMPLISHMENTS AND FUNCTIONS OF THE INSURANCE DEPARTMENT 

1963 The Insurance Department is charged with the duty and responsibility of administering Arizona Revised Statutes, Title 20, consisting of 331 pages of detailed insurance laws. The provisions thereof cover more areas of regulation and supervision than can be beneficially summarized here. But the Department's principal accomplishments and functions during the 1963 calendar year and the 1963-1964 fiscal year were as follows: 

COLLECTION OF TAXES, FEES AND LICENSES 

Premium taxes, together with fees and licenses, collected and remitted to the State Treasurer for the fiscal year amounted to $5,506,520.28. Such constituted an increase of $583,883.23 over collections during the preceding year. 

AUDITS OF INSURER'S ANNUAL STATEMENTS 

Office audits were made of annual statements of all 870 insurers authorized to transact insurance in Arizona. Such audits embraced careful verification of premium tax returns and of financial and operating procedures of every authorized insurer. 

PROCESSING OF INSURER'S APPLICATIONS FOR ADMISSION 

Applications of 75 insurers seeking original authority to transact insurance in Arizona were inspected and reviewed. Based thereon, 40 new authorizations were issued. 

CONDUCT OF FIELD EXAMINATIONS 

Comprehensive field examinations of 32 domestic insurers were conducted by trained and experienced examiners. Every examination was administered in the manner prescribed by the "Manual of Examination Practice and Procedure", a comprehensive guide prepared by the National Association of Insurance Commissioners. 

PROCESSING OF COMPLAINTS AND INQUIRIES 

The investigation and servicing of public complaints against insurance companies, agents, brokers, solicitors and bail bondsmen constitute a vital function of the Insurance Department. 

Complaints processed numbered 931. The Department also responded to a large volume of inquiries and communications received both in Phoenix and Tucson by personal visit (72 visitors daily), by telephone (226 calls daily) and by mail (approximately 500 items daily). 

LICENSING OF AGENTS, BROKERS AND SOLICITORS 

Each person desiring to transact insurance as agent, broker or solicitor must apply for license and be examined. Written examinations were prepared for approximately 2229 individuals for administration on scheduled occasions in Phoenix, Tucson, Yuma and Flagstaff. 

Based thereon, 1696 licenses were issued.

APPROVAL OF FORMS AND FILING OF RATES 

Every life and disability insurer must file with the Director and obtain approval of each policy, rider, endorsement and application form issued in Arizona and every property and casualty insurer must file each rate schedule used. Approximately 10,500 life and disability forms were reviewed and 5,000 fire and casualty rate schedules filed.

LIQUIDATION AND DISSOLUTION OF ARIZONA INSURERS 

1937 - 1964 Even though Arizona is the home of more insurance corporations than any other state excepting one, the number of domestic insurers that have failed, thus requiring liquidation and eventual dissolution is among the lowest in the country only seven during the last twenty-seven years. Liquidations are commenced by the Director upon application to the 

Maricopa County Superior Court which then appoints the Director as receiver, serving under the court's supervision. The seven companies subjected to such proceedings were: 

UNION RESERVE LIFE INSURANCE COMPANY (1938) 

On March 3, 1938, Union Reserve Life Insurance Company, a legal reserve life insurer, was placed in liquidation. The company's assets consisted mostly of mortgages of grossly inflated value. On August 20, 1945, the company was dissolved and the receivership closed. 

TRANS-PACIFIC INSURANCE COMPANY (1955) 

On September 7, 1955, Trans-Pacific Insurance Company, a legal reserve casualty insurer, was placed in liquidation. Proceedings were slowed considerably by contested claims and complicated litigation. Finally, however, on December 28, 1962 the company was dissolved and the receivership closed. 

NATIONAL SECURITY LIFE INSURANCE COMPANY (1956) 

On November 8, 1956, National Security Life Inusrance Company, a limited capital stock insurer was placed in liquidation. Assets were quickly marshalled and no stockholders' assessment was necessary. All claims approved by the court were paid one hundred cents on the dollar, and on May 8, 1957 the company was dissolved and the receivership closed. 

INSURED SAVINGS INSURANCE COMPANY (1956) 

On November 30, 1956, Insured Savings Insurance Company, a mutual benefit insurer, was placed in liquidation. Approved claims were paid ninety cents on the dollar, and on 

August 16, 1957 the company was dissolved and the receivership closed. 

PHOENIX LIFE INSURANCE, INC. (1960) 

On July 26, 1960, Phoenix Life Insurance, Inc., a limited capital stock insurer, was placed in liquidation. Assets were quickly marshalled and no stockholders' assessment was necessary. All claims approved by the court were paid one hundred cents on the dollar, and on June 1, 1961 the company was dissolved and the receivership closed. 

COSMOPOLITAN LIFE INSURANCE COMPANY (1962) 

On May 22, 1962, Cosmopolitan Life Insurance Company, a mutual benefit insurer, was placed in liquidation. Assets were quickly marshalled and all creditors paid one hundred cents on the dollar. Dissolution of the company and closing of the receivership are pending. 

PACIFIC SOUTHWEST LIFE INSURANCE COMPANY (1963) 

On April 22, 1963, Pacific Southwest Life Insurance Company, a legal reserve life insurer, was placed in liquidation. Presently assets and liabilities are being determined and every effort is being extended to minimize losses and to expedite proceedings.

REPORT OF OFFICE OF COMMISSIONER OF PUBLIC BUILDING SAFETY 

There follows a brief outline of activities of the Commissioner of Public Building Safety since June, 1963.

The effective date (June 1962) of legislation that created such office under the Director of Insurance. A.R.S. Sections 20-1701 through 20-1709 set forth the duties and functions of the Commissioner and his office.

The fire chiefs of all organized fire districts have been appointed deputy commissioners and have been furnished a copy of the law. These men have been encouraged to contact the 

Department for information and assistance when needed. 

A membership in the National Fire Protection Association has been effected to assist in performance of important functions. The Association distributes to members data on modern methods of fire safety and circulates pamphlets on safety devices and on fire protection and electrical equipment. Its publications have served as invaluable guides to fire chiefs and other interested in fire protection and safety throughout the United States and Canada. 

The office has received many requests for information from deputy commissioners and from private citizens. Most of these have been relative to the installation and maintenance of private fire protection equipment, standard construction of motion picture projection booths, and location and adequacy of exits. Questions have been answered and standard specifications, diagrams and other data have been supplied to assist in solving these various problems.

Whenever requested, special trips have been made to the fire protection districts to attend meetings or to conduct inspections.

The State Department of Health has requested information relative to exits, the installation of standard first aid for incipient fires and the installation and maintenance of automatic sprinkler equipment. Information has been furnished the inspector for the Department of Health to assist in making inspections.

The Department has cooperated with city fire prevention bureaus on various subjects relative to the prevention of fire.

All correspondence or queries mailed to the State of Arizona, attention State Fire Marshall, are processed in this Department. This requires considerable time, as many questions are asked relative to equipment and the standards as required in the State of Arizona. 

The Department of Public Building Safety was requested by Colonel Jesse G. Holloway 

(Ret.), State Director, Department of Civil Defense, State of Arizona, to furnish a member to act on the "Arizona Rural Fire Defense Plan" Committee. A member of this Department attends all committee meetings, and participates in his particular field. The Department furnished the chairman of the above mentioned committee all information available on the organized fire departments of this State consisting of the number of engines, hook and ladder trucks, hose carts and feet of hose in actual service, the number of organized companies, the systems of water supply in use, and the number of members, with their names, dates of appointment and dates of expiration of their terms. This data furnished the committee on special prepared blanks, is valuable information in planning for rural fire defense. 

G. A. BUSHNELL Commissioner of Public Building Safety By: C. W. LORD Chief Deputy Commissioner

REPORT ON COLLECTIONS FOR EXPENSE RESERVE OF STATE COMPENSATION FUND 

1963 In accordance with the decision in Industrial Commission of Arizona vs. Hartford Accident and Indemnity Co., 144 Pac. 2nd 548 (1943), premium taxes that are collected from casualty insurers upon workmen's compensation writings are remitted by the Insurance Department to the State Treasurer for Deposit in the expense reserve of the state compensation fund. In 1963 such taxes totalled $31,511.59, an increase of $1,357.51 over the $30,154.08 collected in 1962. The 1963 collections were:

REPORT ON COLLECTIONS FOR FIREMEN'S RELIEF AND PENSION FUND 

1963 A.R.S. Section 20-224, by reference to A.R.S. Sections 9-951 and 9-952, provides that the net premium taxes collected each year by the Director on fire insurance shall be apportioned to each town or district having a fire company in accordance with premiums received on properties therein and supplemented by a pro rata share of premium taxes collected on properties outside all such towns or districts and provides further that all such sums shall be remitted by the Director to the State Treasurer for deposit in the firemen's relief and pension fund. In 1963 such taxes totalled $349,647.93, which was $2,043.99 more than the $347,603.94 collected in 1962. The 1963 taxes were duly apportioned as follows: Apportionment

REPORT ON COLLECTIONS FOR HIGHWAY PATROL RETIREMENT FUND 

1963 A.R.S. Section 20-224.01 provides that, beginning on July 1, 1959, every foreign or alien insurer each year shall pay to the Director for remission to the State Treasurer a tax of 1/2 of 1 per cent of net premiums on vehicle insurance and provides further that such sum shall be appropriated to the state retirement system board for deposit in the state highway patrol retirement fund. The 1963 collections totalled $302,239.87 which was $31,777.74 more than the $270,462.13 collected in 1962. Year 1948 $ 5,104.53

Insurance Company, December 31, 1963.

Insurance Department April 17, 1963, cease and desist order issued by Arizona Insurance Department, April 26, 1963.

Insurance Company of New York, December 31, 1963.

Corporation, August 21, 1963.

Company, January 1, 1963.

Insurance Company, November 12, 1963.

Phoenix, Arizona, June 30, 1963.

December 31, 1963. 17. 18. 19. 20. 21.
